| --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-03 | Created | add-requirement |
| 2026-09-03 | Status -> implemented | Serialized flushToDisk through a promise chain; snapshot now taken at execution time so a caller's completion implies its own change reached disk. Regression matches the HEAD baseline failure-for-failure. |


### REL-BGSTAB-021 — A rejected store write rolls back the session and the tab that addTab created

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | reliability |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | Node:test unit suite that injects a store-write failure — the store points at a directory that was never created for the single-call cases, and only the first writeStateToDisk is failed for the concurrent case — then asserts through a stub session manager that the created session was terminated and no other was, that the tab is gone and the active tab is re-chosen, that two pre-existing tabs with live sessions are untouched, that a concurrent add's tab and activation survive, and that no pending terminal title remains armed for the dropped session. Two boundary controls sit in the same file: a successful add must terminate nothing, and a failing terminate must not replace the store error. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

`WorkspaceService.addTab` shall leave no trace of a failed call: when the store write it performs after creating the PTY is rejected, that PTY shall be terminated, the tab it created shall be dropped from the in-memory state without disturbing anything the call does not own, and the store failure shall reach the caller unchanged.

Measured 2026-09-03 against commit `a19ef0d`, the last revision before this fix. In that source `createSession` runs at `WorkspaceService.ts:562` and `await this.save(true)` at `:614`, with no `try`/`catch` between them. Pointing `dataFilePath` at a directory that was never created makes `save(true)` reject with `ENOENT`; `addTab` then rejects, but the stub session manager still holds the session it created and `state.tabs` still holds the tab. Six of the eight cases in `WorkspaceServiceAddTabRollback.test.ts` are red against that source, the first two reading "the PTY created by the failed addTab is still running" and "the tab from the failed addTab is still in the in-memory state".

The concurrency trigger for this is already gone. REL-BGSTAB-020 serialized `flushToDisk`, so two overlapping flushes no longer reject each other and this defect can no longer be reproduced by issuing concurrent calls. The ordering is unchanged, so a genuine I/O failure — a full disk, a denied permission, a deleted data directory — still opens it. Reproduction therefore requires injecting a write failure, not concurrency.

Rollback scope is a decision, not an obvious consequence. `addTab` mutates four things before it saves: it pushes the tab, sets `activeTabId` when the workspace had none, increments `colorCounter`, and stamps `updatedAt`. Only the first two are undone. Dropping the tab while leaving `activeTabId` naming it would produce a workspace whose active tab does not exist, which is worse than the leak being fixed. `colorCounter` and `updatedAt` are deliberately left alone: skipping one colour and holding a newer timestamp in memory are harmless, and undoing them is not.

Undo what the call still owns, never a snapshot. Restoring `activeTabId`, `colorCounter` or `updatedAt` from values captured before the `await` erases whatever a concurrent `addTab` did during it. Measured with two overlapping adds on one workspace where only the first one's write was rejected: the snapshot restore left `activeTabId` null although the surviving tab was there, rolled `colorCounter` back over a colour already handed out, and discarded the surviving call's `updatedAt`. The tab is therefore removed by id, and `activeTabId` is re-chosen from the tabs that survive — the way `deleteTab` picks one — and only when it still names the tab being dropped.

The rollback must also be no *wider* than the call, and that half is easy to leave untested. A fixture holding only the tab under test cannot tell a correct rollback from one that clears every tab or terminates every session, because there is no neighbour to observe: both of those mutants pass such a fixture. Nor can a fixture whose active tab is `tabs[0]` detect a re-pick that runs unconditionally. The suite therefore keeps two pre-existing tabs with live sessions and makes the second one active.

Trap — the cleanup itself can fail. `terminateSession` is async and may reject. The caller needs the store failure, not the cleanup failure: a `catch` that lets the terminate rejection escape replaces the diagnosis with a symptom. The tab-restart path at `WorkspaceService.ts:784-792` in commit `a19ef0d` already solves this by logging `cleanupError` through `console.warn` and rethrowing the original error, and this requirement follows that shape rather than inventing a second one.

Trap — a pending terminal title outlives the session it was armed for. The tab is pushed into `state.tabs` before the store write starts, so a title the shell emits while that write is in flight passes `isTerminalTitleEligible` and arms a debounce timer keyed by session id. Removing the tab does not disarm it. That is usually harmless — when the timer fires, `commitTerminalTitle` finds no tab and returns — but `addTab` accepts a caller-supplied `currentSessionId`, issued by `preallocateMcpSession` and passed on by `addTabWithLaunchContext`, so a caller that retries with the same id gets a new tab wearing the failed attempt's title with `nameSource` flipped to `terminal-title`. Measured: the retried tab's name changed from `Terminal-1` to the earlier attempt's title. The rollback therefore calls `cancelPendingTerminalTitle`, as `deleteTab` and `restartTab` already do.

Decision — the cleanup passes `reason: 'tab-delete'` rather than adding a new `SessionCleanupReason` value. `shouldPersistSessionFinalization` admits only `'process-exit'` and `'direct-session-delete'`, so `'tab-delete'` does not run `markSessionFinalized` against a tab the rollback has already removed from the state. A reason outside that pair would, so anyone changing it must re-check that predicate first.

Out of scope — the dropped tab can still reach the disk. `flushToDisk` serializes writes, so a write queued behind the failing one snapshots the state before this rollback runs; measured with two overlapping adds, the surviving call persisted a store that still held the rolled-back tab while memory held only its own. The pre-fix code left that tab in memory as well, so what the store ends up containing is unchanged by this requirement — what is new is that memory and disk disagree until the next successful save, which any later workspace mutation performs. A restart before that leaves `checkOrphanTabs` to recover a tab the user never kept, exactly as it did before this fix. Closing it requires the rollback to re-enter the flush chain, which is a separate design.

Rejected alternative — saving before creating the PTY. The tab record carries `sessionId` and `currentSessionId`, so there is nothing to persist until the session exists. Reopen only for a design in which the tab is persisted without a session id and bound to one afterwards.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Given an addTab call whose store write is rejected, when the call returns, then the PTY session it created has been terminated and no other session has been terminated.
- [x] AC-2: Given an addTab call whose store write is rejected, when the call returns, then the tab it created is absent from the in-memory state, activeTabId no longer names that tab, and no other tab in the workspace has been removed.
- [x] AC-3: Given an addTab call whose store write is rejected, when the rollback itself fails to terminate the session, then the caller still receives the original store failure rather than the cleanup failure — an implementation that swallowed the store error would otherwise satisfy AC-1 and AC-2.
- [x] AC-4: Given two concurrent addTab calls on the same workspace of which only the first one's store write is rejected, when both have settled, then the second call's tab survives and is the active tab — a rollback that restores workspace state captured before its own await would fail this criterion.
- [x] AC-5: Given an addTab call that armed a pending terminal-title update before its store write was rejected, when the call returns, then no pending title remains for the session it created, so a later tab reusing that session id cannot inherit it.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | server/src/services/WorkspaceServiceAddTabRollback.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5 | 8 pass / 0 fail. Ten control mutants, none surviving, each red only on its intended case, sha256 restore verified after every run: removing the whole catch block (equivalent to the pre-fix source) kills six; removing the terminate call kills three; dropping the tab removal kills four; dropping the active-tab re-pick kills two; dropping the rethrow kills seven; clearing every tab kills two and terminating every session kills one, both only through the neighbour case; replacing the re-pick with a pre-await snapshot kills only the concurrency case; dropping the activeTabId ownership guard kills only the neighbour case, which needs an active tab that is not tabs[0]; and dropping the pending-title cancel kills only the title case. Regression: cwd=server, npx tsx src/test-runner.ts gives 18 failures, the same set as the HEAD baseline measured by restoring only this file with git checkout; npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json exits 0. |

#### Trace Links

| Type | Reference | Relation | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

#### Research / Analysis

- -

#### Implementation Notes

- -

#### Change Notes

| Date | Change | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-03 | Created | add-requirement |
| 2026-09-04 | Status -> implemented | Rollback added to addTab: the tab is removed by id, activeTabId is re-chosen from the survivors the way deleteTab does it, the pending terminal title is cancelled, the session is terminated with the cleanup error swallowed, and the store failure is rethrown. Shared workspace counters are deliberately not restored. Regression matches the HEAD baseline failure-for-failure. |


### OPS-BGSTAB-008 — The canonical authority digest survives checkout line-ending conversion

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | operational |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | Node:test unit suite that reads the digest-covered files from the working tree, recomputes their sha256, and compares against current.json; asserts that git reports the -text attribute for that path so the guarantee is enforced by checkout rather than by luck; runs the bundle writer and asserts it exits 0; and, as a boundary control, alters one byte in a temporary copy and asserts the writer still rejects it. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The sha256 digests recorded in `docs/analysis/terminal-fairness-authority/current.json` shall match the bytes that a checkout of this repository actually produces, so that `server/tools/write-fair-scheduler-evidence-bundle.mjs` completes on every platform. That script runs inside `npm run build` for the server, so a mismatch fails the build and therefore every release target.

Measured 2026-09-06 on Windows. The repository tracks **no `.gitattributes` at all** (`git ls-files | grep -c gitattributes` returns 0) and `core.autocrlf` is `true`, so the 379 files under `docs/analysis/terminal-fairness-authority/` are checked out with CRLF while the digests in `current.json` were computed over LF bytes. All three pointer digests match the git blob (LF) exactly and none match the file on disk:

| file | LF blob sha256 | on disk |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `fair-scheduler-decision.json` | `9a93d118…5533eb` — matches pointer | `0da77dea…74df89` |
| `provenance.json` | `e7689545…e3bd60` — matches pointer | differs |
| `raw/manifest.json` | `5f66f5c7…3d1e7c` — matches pointer | differs |

The build fails at `write-fair-scheduler-evidence-bundle.mjs:133` with `Error: canonical authority digest mismatch`. `tsc` and the provenance step before it both pass, so this is not a type or source error. Stripping the CR bytes from those 379 files — after which `git diff` reports no content change, confirming the bytes now equal HEAD — makes the same build succeed with `fileCount: 19` and exit 0.

Decision — fix this at the checkout layer with `.gitattributes`, not by normalising line endings inside `readCanonicalAuthority` before hashing. That digest exists to detect tampering with the evidence bundle; editing content before hashing makes the check blind to exactly that much tampering, and the same normalisation would have to be propagated to the 15 trial files named by `raw/manifest.json`, loosening the whole verification surface. A checkout-layer problem belongs at the checkout layer.

Trap — adding `.gitattributes` does not repair a working tree that is already checked out. With the `-text` form the blobs are already LF so the commit carries no content change, but each existing checkout still holds CRLF until `git add --renormalize .` or a re-checkout of that path runs there. This repository has many sibling worktrees, and each one carries its own working tree.

Rejected alternative — committing the files with CRLF and recomputing the digests. That pins the digests to one platform's checkout behaviour, so the build would then fail on Linux and macOS runners instead. Reopen only if the evidence bundle stops being consumed on more than one platform.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Given a checkout of this repository on a machine where core.autocrlf is true, when the files under docs/analysis/terminal-fairness-authority/ are read, then their bytes match the sha256 digests recorded in current.json.
- [x] AC-2: Given that working tree, when `node server/tools/write-fair-scheduler-evidence-bundle.mjs` runs, then it completes without a canonical authority digest mismatch.
- [x] AC-3: Given the same working tree with one byte of a digest-covered file altered, when the same script runs, then it still fails with a digest mismatch — a fix that made the check tolerate content differences would otherwise satisfy AC-1 and AC-2.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | server/tools/canonical-authority-line-endings.test.mjs | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3 | 4 pass / 0 fail via `npm run test:release-pipeline`. The fix is a .gitattributes rule pinning docs/analysis/terminal-fairness-authority/** to -text, plus a renormalised working tree. Independently confirmed: all 379 files under that path now hold zero CR bytes and are byte-identical to their HEAD blobs, and the three digests in current.json match the files on disk. Control mutant: deleting the -text rule turns only the git check-attr case red — the digest cases stay green because this working tree is already LF, which is exactly why that case exists. |

| Type | Reference | Relation | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

- -

- -

| Date | Change | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-06 | Created | add-requirement |
| 2026-09-06 | Status -> implemented | Added .gitattributes pinning the authority tree to -text and renormalised this working tree. The bundle writer now completes and the three current.json digests match the files on disk. Fixed at the checkout layer as decided; the digest still rejects a one-byte content change. |


### OPS-BGSTAB-009 — The portable release verifier reads the evidence layout the bundle writer actually produces

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | operational |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | Node:test unit suite that builds evidence-bundle fixtures in a temp directory and calls the verifier directly: one well-formed bundle in the writer's layout asserted to pass, one fixture per required file removed asserted to fail, a path-escape fixture asserted to be rejected, and an assertion that the canary path and the 15-sidecar count are still exercised. The end-to-end confirmation is a portable build for one target completing past the evidence step. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

`tools/build-portable-runtime.js` shall verify the fair-scheduler evidence bundle in the layout that `server/tools/write-fair-scheduler-evidence-bundle.mjs` produces, so that a portable release build reaches its archive step. Today it does not, and every one of the five release targets fails.

Measured 2026-09-06. With the line-ending defect worked around so the build gets this far, `npm run build` dies with `[portable-build] Failed: server\dist\benchmarks\fair-scheduler-evidence\fair-scheduler-decision.json.publication.json missing`.

The two sides assume different producers. The verifier at `:205` and `:265` expects a flat layout whose entry point is `fair-scheduler-decision.json.publication.json`, a pointer carrying `artifactPath` and `rawPath`. That pointer is written by `server/src/benchmarks/terminalFairnessCharacterization.ts:1985` — the benchmark runner. The release build never runs the benchmark; it runs the bundle writer, which copies already-committed evidence out of `docs/analysis/terminal-fairness-authority/` and reproduces that directory's own shape: `current.json` at the root carrying `generation_id`, `decision_artifact`, `provenance_artifact` and `raw_root`; then `generations/<generation_id>/` holding `fair-scheduler-decision.json` (which carries `policy` and a 15-entry `rawEvidencePaths`), `provenance.json`, `raw/manifest.json`, and `raw/fair-scheduler-raw/clients-{1,2,8}/trial-{0..4}.json`.

Nothing is missing from the evidence. The writer's reported `fileCount: 19` is exactly the 15 trials the verifier wants plus `current.json`, the decision, the provenance and the raw manifest. Only the packaging differs, and the verifier's required-paths list at `:265` fails before its parsing logic at `:205` is ever reached.

The two sides drifted apart between releases. `7d07d07` (2026-07-27) added the verifier; `5918146` (2026-08-31) changed the writer. The last release tag is `0.5.3` (2026-05-19), so no release build ran in between and nothing caught it.

Decision — move the verifier to the writer's layout, rather than making the writer also emit a flat pointer. The writer is the later design, and it is the side that validates its own output through the three sha256 digests in `current.json` and keeps generations immutable. Emitting both layouts would record the same fact in two places, which is what drifted here in the first place.

The required data is all reachable without weakening any check. `current.json` gives `generation_id` and `decision_artifact`, which locate the same artifact the old two-hop pointer chain led to, and that artifact still carries `policy` for `validatePublishedFairDeliveryCandidateArtifact` and `rawEvidencePaths` for the count check.

Trap — `rawEvidencePaths` entries are relative to `generations/<id>/raw/`, not to the evidence root. An entry reads `fair-scheduler-raw/clients-1/trial-0.json`, which resolves under `raw/` and nowhere else. Resolving them against the evidence root as the old code did would report every sidecar as missing.

Trap — `generation_id` becomes part of a filesystem path. It arrives from a JSON file inside the artifact, so it must go through the same containment check as the other paths; otherwise a crafted bundle could point outside the evidence root, which is precisely what `resolvePortableFairSchedulerEvidencePath` exists to prevent.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Given a portable build whose evidence bundle was produced by write-fair-scheduler-evidence-bundle.mjs, when validatePortableFairSchedulerEvidenceBundle runs against the output directory, then it completes without error and the build reaches its archive step.
- [x] AC-2: Given that same bundle, when the verifier runs, then it still executes the runtime canary against the artifact's policy field and still requires exactly 15 raw evidence sidecars to be present.
- [x] AC-3: Given a bundle with one required file removed — current.json, the decision artifact, the raw manifest, or any one of the 15 sidecars — when the verifier runs, then it fails; a verifier rewritten to accept the new layout by checking less would otherwise satisfy AC-1 and AC-2.
- [x] AC-4: Given a bundle whose current.json names a generation_id or artifact path that resolves outside the evidence root, when the verifier runs, then it rejects the bundle rather than reading that path.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | tools/build-portable-runtime-evidence.test.mjs | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4 | 6 pass / 0 fail via `npm run test:release-pipeline`, and `npm run build:windows-amd64` completes to `[portable-build] Done (win-amd64)`. Independent verification confirmed the move removed no check and added one: every path — generation, artifact, raw, sidecar — still goes through resolvePortableFairSchedulerEvidencePath, the 15-sidecar count is checked at both the 14 and 16 boundaries, the canary is still spawned, and a raw-manifest existence check is new. The six path-escape fixtures were each shown to be refused by the intended guard before any file was read (generation path invalid x3, artifact path, raw path, sidecar 0). Five control mutants each turn only their own case red: dropping the generation_id guard kills the escape case; resolving sidecars against the evidence root instead of raw/ (the old bug) kills four; weakening the count check kills the 15-sidecar case; dropping the raw-manifest check kills the removal case; ignoring the canary result kills the canary case. None survived; sha256 restore verified after each. |

A raw-manifest existence check was added; nothing was removed. The daemon fixture that encoded the old flat layout was migrated with it, which also fixed a case that had been red since 5918146. |


### OPS-BGSTAB-010 — The published authority generation pins the sources that are actually shipped

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | operational |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | Node:test unit suite that recomputes the pinned-source digest the way the provenance writer does and asserts equality with the artifact current.json points at, and that loads the compiled canary and asserts it accepts the artifact policy. A boundary control alters one pinned source in a temporary copy and asserts the digest then differs. End-to-end confirmation is a portable build for one target completing past the evidence preflight. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The `sourceDigest` carried by the published fair-scheduler authority generation shall equal the digest computed from the sources that ship with it, so that `validatePublishedFairDeliveryCandidateArtifact` accepts the artifact. While they disagree the fair scheduler is inactive at runtime and the portable release build cannot complete.

Measured 2026-09-06 on `feature/markdown-editor`, whose HEAD carries none of the pinned sources as a change. `server/dist/benchmarks/fair-scheduler-source-provenance.json` reports `sourceDigest` `7926df59…c78a3e`; the artifact at `docs/analysis/terminal-fairness-authority/generations/cf56d5d0…96b2ff/fair-scheduler-decision.json` carries `0fc08073…f16af8`. Running the canary against the built bundle returns `{accepted: false, reason: 'decision-artifact-source-digest-mismatch'}`, and the portable build stops with `compiled fair scheduler evidence rejected by portable preflight`.

`write-fair-scheduler-source-provenance.mjs` pins six sources, all under `server/src/`: `benchmarks/terminalFairnessCharacterization.ts`, `benchmarks/fairSchedulerAuthorityLocator.ts`, `ws/wsSendPolicy.ts`, `ws/WsRouter.ts`, `services/TerminalResourcePolicy.ts`, `services/TerminalResourcePolicyCanary.ts`. It digests the working tree directly and makes no git call, so the operand is what is on disk, not what is committed. Commit `5918146` (2026-08-31) changed five of the six and `5f891dc` (2026-08-31) changed `WsRouter.ts`; neither republished the generation, and the last release tag `0.5.3` predates both.

The runtime consequence is worse than the build failure and is what makes this high risk. `WsRouter` answers the terminal-delivery capability handshake with `accepted: false` when the canary rejects, so the fair scheduler is silently off on every connection — no error is raised and no log line names the cause. That has been the state of `origin/main` since 2026-08-31.

Recovery is republishing the generation, not refreshing the provenance file. Running the provenance writer alone makes the digest-equality assertion pass while leaving the artifact's own `sourceDigest` stale, which moves the failure rather than removing it: the canary then rejects for this same reason. The decision artifact has to carry the new digest, and the only way it can is for the benchmark to run again — the measurement is the artifact, so there is no repackage path. `publishFairSchedulerAuthorityGeneration` in `server/src/benchmarks/terminalFairnessCharacterization.ts` is the only entry point; it is deterministic, reproducing an unchanged tree's generation id exactly and verifying rather than overwriting an existing immutable generation.

Trap — the authority tree is tracked. Earlier notes in this project record `docs/analysis/terminal-fairness-authority/` as untracked, which would mean a republish changes no tracked file. It tracks 379 files today, so a republish adds a generation directory and rewrites `current.json` as ordinary tracked changes that must be committed.

Trap — the benchmark burns real CPU even though it runs in virtual time. Do not run it while a latency-sensitive test is measuring on the same machine: the resulting CPU starvation produces a fails-and-resyncs result indistinguishable from a real fault.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Given the working tree that ships in the release, when write-fair-scheduler-source-provenance.mjs computes the source digest, then it equals the sourceDigest carried by the artifact that current.json points at.
- [x] AC-2: Given the built portable bundle, when validatePublishedFairDeliveryCandidateArtifact runs against the artifact policy, then it returns accepted, and a portable build for one target completes past its evidence preflight.
- [x] AC-3: Given any one of the six pinned sources altered by a single byte, when the provenance digest is recomputed, then it no longer equals the artifact's sourceDigest — a repair that made the comparison tolerant would otherwise satisfy AC-1 and AC-2.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | server/src/benchmarks/FairSchedulerAuthorityGenerationPin.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3 | 3 pass / 0 fail via `npm run test:release-pipeline`. Written before the republish and confirmed red first, with AC-2 failing for exactly the reason this requirement records: decision-artifact-source-digest-mismatch. The republish produced generation eebdc9f6…1a57a carrying sourceDigest 7926df59…c78a3e, which two independent recomputations of the six pinned sources both reproduce; the stale side was the artifact, not the provenance. Determinism was measured rather than assumed: a second republish returned the same generation id, added no directory, and reproduced all 18 files byte-for-byte, and none of the 21 pre-existing generations changed (21 -> 22, with current.json the only modified file). The workload arguments were cross-checked against three independent sources — the previous artifact's own workload block, validateInput's hard-coded constraints, and FairSchedulerSourceProvenanceRuntime.test.ts:41-47 — which agree. AC-3 guards against vacuity twice: it asserts the unmutated copy reproduces the published digest before mutating, and asserts the mutation actually changed the file. The six pinned sources were left untouched, confirmed by sha256 before and after. |

AC/Status 불변. |


### REL-BGSTAB-022 — Store publication must not collide between processes sharing one checkout

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | reliability |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | planned |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, persistence, concurrency, feasibility:high |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | node:test unit suites run with `npx tsx --test` from cwd=server/. The cross-instance cases are driven by constructing two independent store instances bound to one data file in a temp directory — separate in-memory state and separate mutation chains over one filesystem, which is what distinguishes them from REL-BGSTAB-020's single-instance fixture. AC-2 and AC-3 are driven deterministically by interposing a peer write on the temp path between a publish's write and its rename, rather than by racing and sampling: a statistical fixture cannot distinguish a publish that moved a peer's bytes from a peer that published afterwards. AC-4, AC-5 and AC-6 are driven by injecting rename failures with specific errno codes at the filesystem boundary, because the transient codes concerned are Windows semantics and are not reproducible natively on the Linux/WSL2 host used; the retry budget is asserted by counting attempts and elapsed waits, not by wall-clock tolerance. Boundary controls assert that a non-transient failure rejects on the first attempt without consuming the budget, that an exhausted budget leaves the pre-existing destination intact and parseable, and that a publish's failure cleanup removes only its own temp file. The characterisation scripts that produced the measurements in the statement are preserved under docs/analysis/kiwi-srs-2026-09-16.buildergate.atomic-store-write/raw/ and are characterisation evidence, not part of the regression suite. |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/BuilderGate/issues/24 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

Every JSON store the server publishes under `server/data/` shall be publishable concurrently by more than one process sharing the checkout without any publish failing because of another publish, without any publish moving a document it did not write into place, and without any publish moving a partially written document into place.

Eight writers persist state under `server/data/`. Seven of them publish JSON stores and all seven are in scope. One, the TOTP secret, is not; it is treated at the end of this paragraph block.

Two of the seven already avoid the shared-temp-path defect: `McpControlConfigStore.ts:68` and `WebhookInvocationService.ts:430` name their temp file per instance, `${dataFilePath}.${process.pid}.${Date.now()}.tmp`. The convention already exists in this codebase and is applied to two of eight writers. They are not, however, already compliant. Neither retries its rename (`McpControlConfigStore.ts:74`, `WebhookInvocationService.ts:436` are bare `await fs.rename` calls), so both remain exposed to the destination contention described under "Why retry is still needed" below, which on Windows fails a publish because of another publish. They need the retry and nothing else, and they are covered by this requirement for that reason.

The remaining five JSON stores need both halves of the fix. Four publish with one identical shape — write `dest + '.tmp'`, copy `dest` to `dest + '.bak'`, rename `dest + '.tmp'` onto `dest` — where the temp path is a fixed string derived from the destination and is therefore the same path in every process that opens the same checkout: `CommandPresetService.ts:270-289`, `WorkspaceService.ts:1726-1755`, `TerminalShortcutService.ts:528-552`, `RecoveryOptionService.ts:410-437`. The fifth of these, `AgentLifecycleService.ts:106-113`, has no temp file at all: it writes `agent-command-profiles.json` directly onto the destination, with no rename and no backup, so a reader can observe a half-written document and a process that dies mid-write destroys the store outright. It is the same defect class in a worse form.

The eighth writer is `TOTPService.ts:113`, which writes the encrypted TOTP secret to `data/totp.secret` with a bare `writeFileSync`, no temp file and no rename. It is deliberately **out of scope** here. Its failure mode is not a torn document but divergent credentials — two processes bootstrapping a checkout with no secret each generate one, and the loser keeps an in-memory secret that no longer matches the file — which needs a decision about which process's secret wins and how the other reloads, not just an atomic publish. It is also a credential, so routing it through a temp file raises a separate question about what a stray temp file would expose. It is written once at first setup rather than on every flush, so it does not share the every-bootstrap exposure that motivates this requirement. Reopen it as its own requirement.

Measured on 2026-09-16, node v24.21.0 on Linux/WSL2. Two failures were reproduced, and a third was looked for and found in a different form than first assumed.

**The publish fails.** Two independent processes each performing the `CommandPresetService` publish sequence 300 times against one shared temp path, writing payloads of 200 and 340 bytes: 293 of 600 publishes (48.8%) failed with `ENOENT` on the rename, because the peer's rename had already moved the shared temp file away. Reproduced twice with the same total.

**A publish moves a document it did not write.** This one is deterministic, not statistical. A publish writes the shared temp file; a peer overwrites that same temp file before the first publish reaches its rename; the rename then moves the peer's bytes into place and the first call resolves with no error. Reproduced directly: writer A wrote `A-change` to the temp path, writer B overwrote the temp path with `B-change`, A's rename resolved successfully, and the destination held `B-change`. B's own rename then failed `ENOENT` against a temp file A had already consumed. The caller was told its write succeeded and its change was not on disk.

**A publish moves a partially written document.** Not because a single `fs.writeFile` splits into several `write` calls — for payloads below Node's 512 KiB chunk threshold it does not — but because each writer opens the shared temp path with `O_TRUNC` and writes from offset zero. A shorter document written over a longer one leaves the shorter document's bytes at the head and the longer one's tail beyond them, and a rename then publishes that. Measured with two processes on one temp path, one writing a 423-byte document and one a 60,023-byte document, the publisher renaming after each write: **539 of 4,000 publishes in the first run and 598 of 4,000 in the second moved a file into place that does not parse as JSON** (13.5% and 14.9%). A published sample is 60,023 bytes long, opens `{"writer":"A","pad":"AAAA…` and ends `…BBBB"}`. Instrumenting the probe to test the mechanism rather than only the symptom (`raw/tear3.mjs`, four runs retained), 2,914 of 2,923 torn publishes across those runs were byte-for-byte the short document followed by the tail of the long one, which is exactly the overlay described. The nine that were not are page-aligned or empty partial views of the longer write — observed lengths 0, 4,096, 8,192, 12,288 and 20,480 — so the overlay is the dominant mechanism rather than the only one. Per-run torn counts were 753, 727, 669 and 674 out of 4,000 publishes each. This is a real corruption path and not a theoretical one, and the destination never has to be read mid-write for it to happen — the rename is atomic and still publishes a broken document, because the bytes it moves were broken before it moved them.

The consequence of the first failure is a dead process rather than a logged warning. `index.ts:1028-1042` awaits `initialize()` on the four temp-file stores during bootstrap. `CommandPresetService.initialize()` and `TerminalShortcutService.initialize()` call `flushToDisk()` on every path through them — file loaded, file absent, backup recovered — with no guard. `WorkspaceService.initialize()` and `RecoveryOptionService.initialize()` open a narrower but real window: the first writes on a successful load whenever the sanitize and migration pass changed tab metadata (`WorkspaceService.ts:214-218`, reached through `ensureMcpTabBinding` for any store written by an older build) or the store held no workspace, and the second writes on a successful load when loading produced new diagnostics (`RecoveryOptionService.ts:52-57`). A rejection from any of them propagates to the `catch` at `index.ts:1654-1657`, which logs `[Fatal] Failed to start server:` and calls `process.exit(1)`. Losing the race kills the instance. A dead instance then fails whatever was pointed at it, which on 2026-08-12 was read as unrelated product defects in E2E specs, on ports 2380 and 2402 (GitHub issue #24).

On Windows the same source-side collision also raises `ENOENT` — the losing rename still finds no temp file — but Windows adds a second failure mode the measurements above cannot produce: a rename onto a destination another process holds open is refused with `EPERM`, which is the code GitHub issue #24 reports. `EPERM` is therefore an additional mode on that platform, not a substitution for `ENOENT`, and it is the one that survives giving each writer a private temp name.

The `.bak` copy has the same weakness for the same reason. Two processes running `copyFile` onto one backup path truncate and rewrite it concurrently, and the backup is what `CommandPresetService.recoverFromBackup()` (`:137-152`), `TerminalShortcutService.recoverFromBackup()` (`:182-200`), `RecoveryOptionService.recoverFromBackupOrDefaults()` (`:192-203`) and `WorkspaceService.initialize()` (`:178-195`) read when the primary will not parse. A backup published non-atomically is a recovery path that can itself be unreadable.

Rejected alternative — retry only. It is the cheapest of the three directions the issue lists and it reaches only one of the three failures. Retrying the rename turns the 48.8% `ENOENT` into a slower success, but a publish that moves a peer's bytes — whole or partial — into place raises no error at all, so there is nothing for a retry to observe. Retry is required here for a narrower reason given below, but as a complement to a private temp path, never as a substitute.

Rejected alternative — a cross-process lock file. A dev instance that is killed leaves the lock held, so the design immediately needs liveness detection and a staleness timeout, which is more mechanism than the thing it replaces. It also buys nothing semantically: two instances legitimately hold divergent complete snapshots, so serializing them orders the overwrite without preventing it.

Rejected alternative — a per-instance destination path. This is the direction the issue names as the one that would have avoided the problem, and it is a data-layout change rather than a bug fix: in a deployment the store is meant to be a single source of truth, and splitting it per instance would make two instances of one installation disagree permanently. The paths are in fact separable — `workspaces.json` through `workspace.dataPath` in the config schema (`config.schema.ts:252`, reaching the service at `WorkspaceService.ts:115`), and the other four through a `dataPath` constructor option (`CommandPresetService.ts:14`, `TerminalShortcutService.ts:20`, `RecoveryOptionService.ts:21`, `AgentLifecycleService.ts:79`; `WorkspaceServiceOptions` at `:43-47` has no such field, so that store is separable only through configuration). The asymmetry that `WorkspaceService` alone has no `dataPath` constructor option is not something this fix needs to touch, and it is recorded here so that it is not re-derived later. A private temp name is computed from whatever destination the store resolves, so the collision is closed for that store on exactly the same terms as the other six regardless of how its path was configured. The asymmetry has one consequence and it is confined to test fixtures: a suite binding that store to a temp directory sets the private field, as `WorkspaceServiceFlushConcurrency.test.ts:38` already did before this change. Reopen it only as an ergonomics question about the options object, never as part of this defect.

But no construction site passes an override — `index.ts:1028-1042` for the four temp-file stores and `index.ts:1049` for the agent-profile store — so every process in a checkout resolves the same absolute path, and making separation the fix would mean asking every operator to configure it correctly forever.

Out of scope — the watcher structure. The issue also names the `tsx` watchers in `dev.js` reacting to one source change as a cause. Making the watchers restart less eagerly would narrow the window without closing it, and it would leave the store unsafe for any other pair of processes. A store that cannot be written safely by two processes is the defect; how often two processes happen to write it is not. Reopen separately if watcher restart storms cause harm on their own.

What the correct fix does not do is make concurrent publishes agree. With a private temp name and an atomic rename the outcome across two instances is last-writer-wins over whole documents. That is the right semantics here — each instance owns a complete snapshot and there is nothing to merge — and it is what the existing code already intended. What changes is that a publish either puts its own complete bytes in place or reports failure, and never reports success for a document it did not write.

Why retry is still needed, and how much. A private temp name removes the shared source, but two instances still rename onto the *same destination*, and on Windows that rename is refused with `EPERM` while another process holds the destination open. The publish shall therefore retry a rename that fails with `EPERM`, `EACCES` or `EBUSY` up to four times after the first attempt, waiting 10 ms, 20 ms, 40 ms and 80 ms — five attempts and about 150 ms in total. The budget is fixed here rather than left open because an unspecified budget cannot be tested: an implementation retrying once and one retrying fifty times would both satisfy a criterion that only says "bounded". 150 ms is chosen to cover a peer's brief hold on the destination without adding a visible stall to bootstrap, and it is a starting value open to revision against a Windows measurement, which does not yet exist. The retry shall be selective: an implementation that retried every error would turn a permanent failure such as `ENOSPC` into a delay before the same failure.

REL-BGSTAB-020 is not superseded. It serializes `flushToDisk` behind a promise chain so two flushes inside one process cannot overlap, and it remains correct and necessary for that. A promise chain is per-process state; it cannot observe a second process, and a shared temp path defeats it across the process boundary. This requirement extends it outward and must not weaken it: the in-process chain stays.

Platform honesty. The `EPERM` this issue reports is a Windows rename semantic, and every measurement above was taken on Linux/WSL2, which has no equivalent of it: a rename there is not refused because a peer holds the destination open, so the destination-contention failure could not be produced at all. The source-side collision, which does reproduce here as `ENOENT`, is a different failure and is not a stand-in for it. The retry branch must therefore be verified by injecting the transient codes at the filesystem boundary rather than by a native reproduction. That is weaker evidence, and a Windows run remains the stronger check.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given two independently constructed store instances bound to one data file, when both publish concurrently, then both calls resolve — neither rejects because the other moved or removed the temp file it was using.
- [ ] AC-2: Given a publish whose temp file is overwritten in full by a peer between its write and its rename, when the publish resolves, then the destination holds the document that publish wrote — a publish that resolves having moved a peer's document into place fails this criterion.
- [ ] AC-3: Given a publish whose temp file is partially overwritten by a peer writing a shorter document between its write and its rename, when the publish resolves, then the destination parses and holds the document that publish wrote — an implementation sharing one temp path across writers publishes the shorter document followed by the longer one's tail and fails this criterion.
- [ ] AC-4: Given a publish that fails after its temp file exists, when the call rejects, then only that call's own temp file has been removed and a concurrent publish's in-flight temp file is still present — an implementation that unlinks a shared temp path would otherwise satisfy AC-1.
- [ ] AC-5: Given a destination rename that fails with EPERM, EACCES or EBUSY injected at the filesystem boundary, when fewer than five attempts have been made, then the publish waits and retries — the attempts are one initial plus four retries and the waits are 10 ms, 20 ms, 40 ms and 80 ms; and given a rename that fails with those codes on all five attempts, when the call returns, then it rejected and the destination file that existed beforehand is unchanged and still parses.
- [ ] AC-6: Given a rename that fails with a non-transient code such as ENOSPC, when the call returns, then it rejected on the first attempt without consuming the retry budget — an implementation that retried every error would otherwise satisfy AC-5.
- [ ] AC-7: Given a publish that writes a backup as part of its sequence, when a peer's write of a document of a DIFFERENT length is interposed on the backup path between that publish's backup write and its completion, then the backup file the publish leaves behind parses as a complete document — a backup published by a non-atomic copy onto a shared path fails this criterion. Both the interposition and the length difference are required: a racing fixture does not expose the defect because the shared-path copy usually still settles on a whole document, and an equal-length interposition leaves no foreign tail for the check to catch. A publish that deliberately skips the backup, as RecoveryOptionService does on its recovery paths via preserveExistingBackup, is not required to write one.
- [ ] AC-8: Given each of the seven JSON stores under server/data/ (command presets, workspace, terminal shortcuts, recovery options, agent command profiles, MCP control config, MCP webhook records), when AC-1 through AC-7 are evaluated against it, then each holds — a fix applied only to the command-preset store fails this criterion. The agent-command-profile store, which today writes directly onto its destination, must acquire the same publish sequence including the backup; the two stores that already name their temp file per instance must acquire the retry of AC-5, which neither has today. The TOTP secret file is out of scope per the statement and is not evaluated here.
- [ ] AC-9: Given two overlapping publishes issued through one single instance, when both have settled, then each caller's own change is readable from disk after its call resolves — REL-BGSTAB-020's in-process serialization must survive this change unweakened.

Four evaluator rounds since then inserted the partial-overwrite criterion and split the retry criterion, so the same three controls are now AC-4 (a failing publish must unlink only its own temp file, which an implementation unlinking a shared path would otherwise satisfy via AC-1), AC-6 (a non-transient code must reject on the first attempt, which an implementation retrying everything would otherwise satisfy via AC-5) and AC-7 (the backup). The intent is unchanged and all three are implemented and passing; the numbers are logged here rather than reinterpreted so a later reader is not misled by the mismatch. |

### REL-BGSTAB-023 — Checkpoint snapshot byte budget is its own configuration key

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | reliability |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, wave-3, checkpoint, terminal-resource-policy, config-key, github-issue-25 |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | Config schema 의 range·default·strict 거부를 server unit test 로 검증한다. Frontend runtime-config resolver 가 키 부재·범위 밖·유효 입력에서 각각 default·default·입력값을 내는지, 그리고 그 fallback 이 `visibleOutputQueueMaxBytes` 가 아닌지 unit test 로 검증한다. Production 경로 독립성은 `TerminalView` 가 coordinator 에 넘기는 옵션 집합을 대상으로 한 테스트로 검증하되, `visibleOutputQueueMaxBytes` 와 다른 `checkpointMaxBytes` 가 checkpoint admission arm 에만 도달하고 hold arm 은 불변임을 단언한다. 신규 키의 manifest 등록은 `server/src/services/TerminalResourcePolicy.test.ts` 의 static inventory contract test 와 `tools/wave3/terminal-resource-consumer-manifest.test.mjs` 로 검증한다. 기본값 불변은 schema default 와 frontend default 가 모두 4194304 임을 단언해 검증한다. 재발행 후 `npm --prefix server run test:authority-pin` 이 통과해야 한다. |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/BuilderGate/issues/25 |
| Related Docs | docs/analysis/2026-09-16.issue26-checkpoint-mint-size/00.decision.md, docs/report/2026-09-16.issue26-checkpoint-mint-size-decision.md |

#### Requirement

Checkpoint snapshot 의 byte 예산과 post-checkpoint hold 버퍼의 byte 예산은 서로 다른 예산이므로 각각 별도의 canonical config key 로 관리되어야 한다. Browser write coordinator 의 checkpoint admission 은 운영자가 `resourceLimits.terminal.checkpointMaxBytes` 로 독립 조정할 수 있어야 하며, 값을 주지 않았다는 이유로 hold 예산(`resourceLimits.terminal.visibleOutputQueueMaxBytes`)을 그대로 상속해서는 안 된다.

#### Rationale

오늘 production 경로에서 두 예산은 하나의 숫자다. `frontend/src/utils/terminalWriteCoordinator.ts:430-432` 가 `options.checkpointMaxBytes ?? postCheckpointMaxBytes` 로 해석하고, 유일한 production call site 인 `frontend/src/components/Terminal/TerminalView.tsx:3520-3521` 이 `postCheckpointMaxBytes` 만 넘기기 때문이다. Coordinator 자체는 이미 독립 값을 받는다 — `frontend/tests/unit/terminalWriteCoordinator.test.ts:1247-1272` 가 hold=1 / checkpoint=1048576 로 통과한다. 따라서 이 Requirement 는 coordinator 로직이 아니라 배관(plumbing) 이다. 고정 배수로 유도하는 방식은 기각한다: SGR 바이트는 cell 수에 비례하지 않아 checkpoint payload 에 유도 가능한 상한이 없고, 서버는 mint 시점에 수용 가능성을 검사하지 않고 64KiB 단위로 자르기만 하므로, 상한을 넘는 상황에서 운영자가 설정을 올리는 것이 유일한 대응 수단이다. 유도 상수는 그 대응 수단 자체를 없앤다. Issue #26 의 재측정은 이 결함이 실사용 조건에서 발동함을 보였다 — token 당 SGR 밀도에서 교차점은 166 컬럼이고, 400 컬럼에서는 줄당 SGR run 2개로 한도를 넘는다. 즉 이 키는 테스트 편의용이 아니라 운영자의 유일한 레버다.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `resourceLimits.terminal.checkpointMaxBytes` 는 server config schema 에 자체 canonical key 로 존재해야 하며, 그것이 분리해 내는 `visibleOutputQueueMaxBytes` 와 동일한 범위 [1024, 268435456] 및 동일한 strict-object·silent-coercion-금지 규칙을 따르되, 그 키의 alias 나 파생값이어서는 안 된다.
- [x] AC-2: Production browser 경로는 해석된 terminal limits 의 `checkpointMaxBytes` 를 write coordinator 에 전달해야 한다. `visibleOutputQueueMaxBytes` 와 다른 값을 설정하면 그 값은 checkpoint admission (거부 사유 `invalid-checkpoint-metadata`) 의 byte 예산만 바꾸어야 하고, post-checkpoint hold (거부 사유 `post-checkpoint-hold-overflow`) 의 byte 예산은 `visibleOutputQueueMaxBytes` 로 남아야 한다.
- [x] AC-3: Frontend runtime-config 해석(`frontend/src/utils/inputReliabilityMode.ts`)은 동일한 default 와 범위로 이 키를 운반해야 하며, 키가 없거나 유효하지 않을 때 `visibleOutputQueueMaxBytes` 가 아니라 자기 default 로 수렴해야 한다.
- [ ] AC-4: 신규 키는 `server/src/services/TerminalResourcePolicy.ts` 의 terminal resource key 표와 terminal resource consumer manifest 에 실제 consumer call site 와 함께 등록되어야 한다. 그 등록이 OBS-BGSTAB-005 AC-6 의 static inventory contract test 와 `tools/wave3/terminal-resource-consumer-manifest.test.mjs` 에서 green 으로 닫히려면 두 스위트의 선재 실패가 먼저 해소되어야 하며, 그때까지 이 AC 는 미충족으로 남는다. OBS-BGSTAB-005 의 봉인된 manifest 는 이 Requirement 로 갱신되므로 재검증 사실을 그 Requirement 의 Change Notes 에 남겨야 한다.
- [x] AC-5: 출하 default 는 4194304 로 유지되어 이 Requirement 만으로는 effective runtime 동작이 바뀌지 않아야 한다. 다른 어떤 resource default 도 이 Requirement 로 변경되지 않는다.
- [x] AC-6: Chunk 개수 예산 분리는 이 Requirement 의 범위 밖이다. `checkpointMaxChunks` 는 값이 주어지지 않으면 계속 `postCheckpointMaxChunks` 로 수렴해야 하며, 이 Requirement 는 그 동작을 바꾸지 않는다.
- [x] AC-7: AC-4 로 인해 pinned fair-scheduler source 의 내용이 바뀌어 sourceDigest 가 달라지면, canonical authority generation 을 재발행해 OPS-BGSTAB-010 AC-1 의 sourceDigest pin 이 동결된 트리에서 성립해야 하고, 재검증 사실을 OPS-BGSTAB-010 의 Change Notes 에 남겨야 한다.

#### Research / Analysis

- -

#### Implementation Notes

범위 밖 — chunk 개수 예산 분리: 이것은 TDD 상 불가능해서가 아니라 사용자/오케스트레이터가 정한 범위 결정이다. Byte 와 마찬가지로 `TerminalView.tsx:3521` 이 `postCheckpointMaxChunks` 만 넘기므로 chunk 에 대해서도 production 경로 실패 테스트는 동일하게 작성 가능하다. 그럼에도 분리하지 않는 이유는 issue #25 가 byte 예산 하나로 범위를 한정했고 chunk arm 은 오늘 관측된 장애 경로가 아니기 때문이다. 잔존 위험: 누군가 hold 의 chunk arm 에 도달하려고 `resourceLimits.terminal.visibleOutputMaxChunks` 를 낮추면 같은 값이 snapshot chunk 상한도 함께 낮추고, 거부된 checkpoint 를 서버가 같은 크기로 재발행하는 resync 루프가 재발한다.
절차 비용 1 — fair-scheduler 재발행: `server/src/services/TerminalResourcePolicy.ts` 는 `server/tools/write-fair-scheduler-source-provenance.mjs:7-14` 가 pin 하는 6개 소스 중 하나다. AC-4 를 만족시키려 이 파일을 편집하면 pinned sourceDigest 가 바뀌어 OPS-BGSTAB-010 의 generation pin 이 red 가 되고, canonical authority 재발행(AC-7)으로만 복구된다. 재발행 CLI runner 는 없으며 임시 스크립트로 수행한다.
절차 비용 2 — consumer manifest guard: `tools/wave3/terminal-resource-consumer-manifest.test.mjs` 는 key 표 대조 외에도 하드코딩된 `expectedResourceKeys` 배열, 고정 legacy-manifest sha256, 그리고 lineage 의 `assert.deepEqual` 을 갖는다. 신규 키는 그 세 곳과 `terminal-resource-consumer-manifest.json`/`.current.json`/`.lineage.json` 에도 함께 반영되어야 한다.
Target 선택: 직접 부모인 REL-BGSTAB-007 과 OBS-BGSTAB-005 가 모두 `wave-3` 이고 issue #25 가 "Wave3 후속" 이므로 `wave-3` 을 택했다. AC-7 의 작업 대상인 OPS-BGSTAB-010 은 `0.5.5-buildergate-stability` 에 있어 target 이 교차한다는 점을 기록해 둔다.

AC-2 의 production call site 테스트는 파일 끝까지 slice 해 주석이나 다른 객체 리터럴로도 통과할 수 있었으므로 해당 호출의 옵션 객체로 범위를 좁혔다. |
| 2026-09-16 | 재봉인이 흡수한 소스 해시 churn 의 원인은 줄바꿈이다 (VE-3 보완) | VE-3 은 흡수된 churn 을 relocation 8건·AST 해시 이동 6건으로만 기술했으나, 실제로는 sourceHashes 35개 중 28개가 다시 쓰였고 그중 9개는 이 변경이 건드리지 않은 파일이다. 실측으로 원인을 확정했다 — frontend/src/services/tokenStorage.ts 의 이전 기록값 cded9fb6... 은 CRLF 바이트의 sha256 이고 새 값 90241907... 은 LF 워크트리 바이트의 sha256 이다. frontend/src/utils/terminalOutputHotPath.ts 도 동일하다. 즉 이전 봉인은 CRLF 체크아웃에서, 이번 재봉인은 LF 체크아웃에서 이뤄졌다. 결과적으로 이 봉인은 LF 특정이며 Windows 체크아웃에서 다시 drift 한다. .gitattributes 는 provenance 6개 소스와 docs/analysis/terminal-fairness-authority/** 만 고정하고 manifest 의 evidence 소스 경로는 고정하지 않는다. 같은 원인이 TerminalResourcePolicy.test.ts:1252 의 reserved-copy-option-decoy 실패도 설명한다. 이 취약성의 해소(.gitattributes 고정 또는 git blob 바이트 해시)는 이 Requirement 의 범위 밖이므로 별도 보고했다. |
| 2026-09-16 | 재리뷰 반영 — AC-4 차단 사유 두 가지를 구분한다 | AC-4 를 막는 TerminalResourcePolicy.test.ts 의 실패는 성격이 다른 두 가지다. (1) :1377 의 reserved-copy-option-decoy 는 :1252 fixture 가 CRLF 리터럴을 요구하므로 LF 체크아웃에서는 제품 상태와 무관하게 실패한다 — 환경 산물이다. (2) :924 의 ConfigFileRepository @resource-limits-root.[dynamic] 미등록 call site 는 줄바꿈과 무관하며 CRLF 체크아웃에서도 남는다. 따라서 AC-4 의 체크 해제는 (2) 하나만으로도 성립하고, 앞선 Change Note 가 세 실패를 구분 없이 "선재 결함" 으로 묶은 것은 부정확했다. 이 Requirement 는 CLAUDE.md 가 검증 플랫폼으로 지정한 Windows/CRLF 체크아웃에서 측정되지 않았으므로, 거기서 (1) 이 사라질지는 확인되지 않았다. |
| 2026-09-16 | 정정 — 앞선 소스 해시 churn 기록의 수치와 기전이 틀렸다 | 앞 Change Note 는 리뷰어 수치를 재도출 없이 옮겨 "35 중 28 이 바뀌었고 9 개는 미편집 파일" 이라 적고 churn 을 줄바꿈 탓으로 돌렸다. d33f389^ 대조 재측정 결과 바뀐 것은 23 개이고 그중 14 개는 이 변경이 편집한 파일이다. 미편집 9 개 중 줄바꿈이 원인인 것은 2 개뿐이며(tokenStorage.ts, terminalOutputHotPath.ts) 나머지 7 개는 baseline 에서 이 가드를 red 로 만들던 통상적 내용 drift 다. 줄바꿈 비중을 과장한 앞 기록을 이 항목으로 대체한다. |
| 2026-09-16 | cded9fb6 이 CRLF 다이제스트임을 전 리비전 해싱으로 증명했다 | tokenStorage.ts 와 terminalOutputHotPath.ts 의 git 이력 전 리비전을 해싱했다. 어느 리비전의 blob 도 이전 기록값과 그대로 일치하지 않았고, CRLF 로 변환한 blob 만 일치했으며 그 blob 의 LF 형태가 오늘의 워크트리 내용이다. 따라서 이전 값은 다른 리비전이 아니라 동일 내용의 CRLF 다이제스트다. cded9fb6 = CRLF, 90241907 = LF. |
| 2026-09-16 | 봉인은 LF 특정이 아니라 양방향 플랫폼 의존이며 재봉인은 정정이 아니다 | 다이제스트는 워크트리 바이트를 덮는다(TerminalResourcePolicyInventory.ts:1359). .gitattributes 의 text eol=lf 6줄은 fair-scheduler provenance 소스이고 -text 한 줄은 docs/analysis/terminal-fairness-authority/** 다. consumer manifest 경로는 check-attr 상 unspecified 이며 sourceHashes 35 개 중 고정된 것은 3 개, 32 개는 unspecified 다. 줄바꿈으로 churn 한 2 개는 모두 unspecified 쪽이라 어느 봉인도 고정 규칙을 어기지 않았다. 고정된 WsRouter.ts/wsSendPolicy.ts 가 줄바꿈 아닌 내용으로 churn 한 점이 반대 방향에서도 같은 결론을 준다. 이전 값은 정당한 Windows 봉인, 이번 값은 정당한 WSL 봉인이며 재봉인은 정정도 신규 위험도 아니다. Windows 재drift 는 추론이다 — 이 레인에 Windows 체크아웃이 없어 직접 관측하지 못했다. |

### FR-BGSTAB-027 — Retained checkpoint attribute parity is invariant to SGR palette encoding

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, wave-3, authority-promotion, retained-shadow-comparer, sgr, github-issue-28 |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | Headless terminal round-trip 단위 테스트로 P16/P256 등가성과 비-등가 경계(다른 slot, default, truecolor)를 검증하고, SGR 밀집 콘텐츠 세션의 comparer axes를 회귀 테스트로 확인한다. |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/BuilderGate/issues/28 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

Retained shadow comparer의 cell attribute 투영은 같은 palette slot을 가리키는 서로 다른 SGR encoding(P16 short form과 P256 indexed form, index 0..15)을 동일한 attribute로 취급해야 한다. Checkpoint round-trip이 관측 가능한 terminal state를 보존하면 cells axis는 match여야 하며, SGR 색상이 존재한다는 이유만으로 retained-state parity gate가 실패해서는 안 된다.

#### Rationale

BuilderGate의 핵심 시나리오인 코딩 에이전트 출력은 SGR이 밀집해 있다. @xterm/addon-serialize는 P256 index 0..15를 P16 short form으로 재출력하므로 rehydrate된 cell의 fgColorMode/bgColorMode가 원본과 달라진다. 관측되는 렌더 결과는 동일한데 attributeHash만 달라져 MIG-BGSTAB-002 AC-1의 retained-state parity gate가 영구적으로 실패한다.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: 256-color indexed SGR(ESC[38;5;N 또는 ESC[48;5;N, N=0..15)로 그려진 cell을 담은 retained checkpoint는 round-trip 비교에서 cells axis가 match여야 한다.
- [x] AC-2: 같은 palette slot을 가리키는 P16 short form(ESC[30m..37m, 90m..97m, 40m..47m, 100m..107m)과 P256 indexed form은 attribute 투영에서 동일한 token으로 정규화되어야 한다.
- [x] AC-3: 서로 다른 palette slot, default color, truecolor(RGB)는 정규화 후에도 서로 구별되어야 하며 이 정규화가 실제 attribute 차이를 은폐해서는 안 된다.
- [x] AC-4: SGR 색상이 밀집된 scrollback을 가진 session은 retained-state parity gate를 통과할 수 있어야 하며 retained-state-parity-mismatch blocker가 색상만을 이유로 발생해서는 안 된다.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

RED before the fix: 3 fail / 2 pass — raw/red-20260916T072627Z.log sha256 15c4a0b3f52bacbaba2dc9940a790b77af4cf58683de46b12de5059b9c107035. AC-4의 comparer-axis 수준(cells axis가 match이고 6개 principal axis가 색상만을 이유로 어긋나지 않는다)은 VE-1이 compareRetainedHeadlessCheckpointRoundTrip을 직접 호출해 실행으로 증명한다. 그러나 blocker 문자열 수준(retained-state-parity-mismatch)은 실행으로 관측하지 않았다 — VE-1은 SessionManager.readTerminalAuthorityPromotionParitySnapshot(SessionManager.ts:2928)과 blockers.push(SessionManager.ts:2989-2992)에 도달하지 않으며, 그 구간은 comparer 결과가 blocker 선택으로 이어지는 코드 경로 추적으로만 확정했다. 실제 승급 경로를 띄워 관측하는 live promotion E2E는 미수행 과제로 남아 있다. |

### REL-BGSTAB-024 — Production unified-only transport boundary and split contract deferral

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | reliability |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | planned |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, websocket, transport-boundary, split-deferral, wave-0, github-issue-3, feasibility:high |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | Source-contract characterization tests over the production startup wiring and the transport eligibility predicate, plus the existing split handshake and transport parser suites. No production runtime behaviour is changed, so verification observes the boundary rather than exercising a new code path. |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/BuilderGate/issues/3 |
| Related Docs | docs/analysis/2026-09-16.wave0-split-contract/00.decision-record.md, docs/research/2026-07-15.orca-terminal-stack-absorption-research-and-implementation-plan.ko.md |

#### Requirement

The production WebSocket transport shall route terminal traffic in unified mode only. The configured realtime.wsTransportMode shall not reach the WsRouter routing decision in the production startup wiring; split and split-shadow shall remain a standalone router capability reachable only by explicit router construction outside production; and activation of split runtime shall require a separately approved decision that this requirement does not grant.

#### Rationale

GitHub issue #3 required an explicit XOR between restoring the FR-BGSTAB-006/007 split contract in production (0A) and deferring or superseding it (0B). 0B was selected. The decisive evidence is that the binary data plane, whose adoption is already decided, is gated to the unified transport by isTransportEligible in server/src/ws/terminalWireFormat.ts, so restoring split in production would withdraw an adopted decision. Production has additionally never routed terminal payload over a split socket: server/src/index.ts passes no option named wsTransportMode to the router, and a green assertion in server/src/test-runner.ts pins that omission. The configured value is not inert, however. It reaches production as the binaryNegotiationTransportMode option and as the terminal authority topology selector, which AC-8 records, and an earlier draft of this requirement wrongly described it as withheld from the router altogether. Recording the boundary at that precision is the point: later waves must build on an accurate statement of production behaviour rather than on FR-BGSTAB-006/007, whose verification notes describe a standalone capability as though it were verified in production.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given the production server startup wiring, when WsRouter is constructed in server/src/index.ts, then no option named wsTransportMode is passed to it, so the router's routing transport mode resolves to its literal default of unified. The existing assertion named assert.doesNotMatch(construction, /wsTransportMode/u) in server/src/test-runner.ts observes that absence.
- [ ] AC-2: Given the production wiring of AC-1, when a terminal payload is routed, then it is routed on the unified single-socket path, because the routing decision reads the router's own transport mode and never the configured value.
- [ ] AC-3: Given a caller constructs WsRouter with an explicit realtime.wsTransportMode option outside production, then split and split-shadow handshake and routing behaviour remain available as a standalone capability, and this requirement neither removes that capability nor deletes split code.
- [ ] AC-4: Given the frontend builds a control WebSocket URL through buildControlWebSocketUrl in frontend/src/utils/webSocketUrl.ts, then the transport parameter it emits is named mode while the production WsRouter upgrade path reads a parameter named wsTransportMode, so a browser-built URL cannot select split. This divergence is a limitation recorded by this contract, not a defect resolved by it.
- [ ] AC-5: Given the production WebSocket upgrade path, then the canonical parser parseWsTransportRequest in server/src/ws/wsTransportMode.ts is not invoked, so its split-disabled rejection is not part of production behaviour.
- [ ] AC-6: Given the binary terminal wire format, then it is eligible only while the transport mode is unified, as decided by isTransportEligible in server/src/ws/terminalWireFormat.ts, so restoring split in production would disable the adopted binary data plane.
- [ ] AC-7: Given this requirement, then it does not activate split runtime and does not alter the Status, Stability, or body of FR-BGSTAB-006 or FR-BGSTAB-007; activating split requires a separately approved decision recorded against a future requirement.
- [ ] AC-8: Given the configured realtime.wsTransportMode, then it does reach production by exactly two named paths other than routing: the binaryNegotiationTransportMode option of WsRouter, which gates binary negotiation eligibility, and the transportMode argument of attachProductionTerminalAuthority, which selects the terminal authority topology. Setting it to split therefore changes production behaviour on those two paths while leaving terminal payload on the unified socket, and any claim that the setting is wholly inert in production is false.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | server/src/ws/productionTransportBoundary.test.ts | AC-1..AC-6 | Characterization suite, 8 tests, 8 pass, 0 fail, 0 todo (cwd=server, npx tsx --test src/ws/productionTransportBoundary.test.ts). Sensitivity was demonstrated rather than assumed: adding wsTransportMode to the production WsRouter construction in server/src/index.ts made the AC-1 test fail with its intended message, and reverting restored a clean tree. No red-first commit exists because the requirement contracts an existing boundary, so a characterization test for it is green on first run by construction. The eighth case was added in review round 1 to cover AC-8. |
| VE-2 | artifact | docs/analysis/2026-09-16.wave0-split-contract/00.decision-record.md | AC-7 | Wave 0 decision record: the 0A/0B XOR, the evidence for 0B, the rejected alternatives, the bounded rollback, and the five items reserved for user approval (A-1..A-5). AC-7 is satisfied by this record rather than by code. |
| VE-3 | test | server/src/ws/WsRouterSplitHandshake.test.ts | AC-3 | 2026-09-16 re-measurement at commit ddfb923: tests 28, pass 15, fail 0, todo 13, exit 0. The 15 ordinary passes are the standalone split capability AC-3 preserves; the 13 todo cases are the production-unified limitation, twelve tagged Wave-1 and one Wave-3. Exit code 0 is not a pass signal for this file. Raw log: docs/analysis/2026-09-16.wave0-split-contract/raw/split-handshake-20260916T184330.log sha256 5c13fc79acf933def8a97d66892d16872129823e40e54b1bfddb551ad54c3149. |

Demoting it is an open user decision (decision record item A-1). |

Its VE-1 note asserting that the focused split tests passed is corrected by the evidence rows added to this requirement (decision record item A-1). |

The same file does pass the configured value as binaryNegotiationTransportMode and to attachProductionTerminalAuthority (AC-8). |

#### Research / Analysis

- -

#### Implementation Notes

- Do not wire config.realtime.wsTransportMode into the WsRouter routing mode; the omission is the contract. Two transition metrics, not one, are needed because the configured value reaches production by two other paths (AC-8). First, assert.doesNotMatch(construction, /wsTransportMode/u) in server/src/test-runner.ts: if it fails, a routing switch has entered the production wiring. Second, the binaryNegotiationTransportMode option and the attachProductionTerminalAuthority transportMode argument in server/src/index.ts: if either stops deriving from the configured value, or starts feeding the routing mode, this contract is void. Watching only the first metric would miss an operator setting split and silently losing binary negotiation.

#### Change Notes

| Date | Change | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-16 | Created | add-requirement |
| 2026-09-16 | AC-1 corrected and AC-8 added | Mandatory review round 1 found that AC-1 and the rationale overstated the boundary. The configured realtime.wsTransportMode does reach production: server/src/index.ts reads it into configuredWsTransportMode and passes it as the WsRouter binaryNegotiationTransportMode option and as the attachProductionTerminalAuthority transportMode argument. Only the routing mode is withheld. AC-1 now says so precisely, AC-8 contracts the two live paths, and the implementation notes name two transition metrics instead of one. The 0B decision is unaffected; the boundary it records is now stated at the right precision. |

### FR-BGSTAB-028 — Semantic tuning profiles with a numeric compatibility facade

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | planned |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, settings, semantic-profile, compatibility-facade, conpty, wave-0, github-issue-3, feasibility:high |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | Settings capability tests, SettingsService patch validation tests, RuntimeConfigStore apply-summary tests covering profile-to-numeric resolution and the profile/explicit-key precedence order, and a cross-check against the configuration settings inventory fixture. |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/BuilderGate/issues/3 |
| Related Docs | docs/analysis/2026-09-16.wave0-split-contract/00.decision-record.md, docs/research/2026-06-30.buildergate-conpty-output-recovery-input-lag-implementation-plan.md |

#### Requirement

The Settings and runtime configuration contract shall present terminal transport and recovery tuning as a small set of named semantic profiles rather than as a matrix of individually tuned low-level byte, chunk, and millisecond values. The existing per-key numeric surface shall be retained as a compatibility facade so that configurations written against it keep working and keep their meaning, and keys without a runtime consumer shall remain explicitly unavailable rather than becoming editable through either surface.

#### Rationale

Epic #2 core principle 1 requires user-tuned low-level buffer numbers to converge on meaning-based profiles plus a compatibility facade. FR-BGSTAB-015 predates that principle: it contracted a 29-row per-key numeric Settings matrix, and after the C-4 correction only the recentEventLimit row carries evidence, leaving the remaining rows as an open-ended obligation to expose more raw numbers. Superseding the direction rather than the record keeps the matrix available as the facade while moving the user-facing surface to profiles. FR-BGSTAB-015 is Status=in_progress, so it is not discarded here; retiring it is an open user decision (decision record item A-5).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given the Settings capability contract, when tuning capabilities are loaded, then the user-facing surface offers named semantic profiles whose identifiers describe intent rather than byte, chunk, or millisecond magnitudes, and each profile resolves deterministically to a complete set of the underlying numeric values.
- [ ] AC-2: Given a configuration that sets underlying numeric keys directly, when it is loaded, then the compatibility facade preserves those values and their runtime meaning without requiring the operator to migrate to a profile, and the resolved configuration is observable as such.
- [ ] AC-3: Given a profile is selected and an underlying numeric key is also set explicitly, then the resolution order between them is deterministic, recorded in the apply summary, and observable to the operator rather than silently resolved.
- [ ] AC-4: Given a setting has no runtime consumer, or has exactly one literal behaviour, then it remains explicitly unavailable through both the profile surface and the compatibility facade, and is not made editable by the introduction of profiles. This restates the guarantee FR-BGSTAB-015 AC-5 carried. re_stated_from: FR-BGSTAB-015#AC-5
- [ ] AC-5: Given stabilityModes.wsSendMode remains direct, when a profile or facade value whose effect depends on safe-send is displayed or saved, then the dependency is observable so the value is not presented as effective. This restates the guarantee FR-BGSTAB-015 AC-4 carried. re_stated_from: FR-BGSTAB-015#AC-4
- [ ] AC-6: Given the classification produced by the configuration settings inventory, then every key the inventory classifies as user-visible is reachable through exactly one of the profile surface or the compatibility facade, and every key it classifies as reserved, inert, or safety-only is reachable through neither.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

FR-BGSTAB-015 is Status=in_progress and is deliberately not discarded here; its matrix is the facade definition. Retiring it is an open user decision (decision record item A-5). |
| Requirement | FR-BGSTAB-013 | extends | Extends the Wave 6 browser Settings contract by replacing the per-key numeric surface with profiles while keeping the reserved-field exclusions intact. |

### OPS-BGSTAB-011 — Machine-readable configuration settings inventory

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | operational |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | planned |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, configuration, settings-inventory, machine-readable, wave-0, github-issue-3, feasibility:high |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | An automated test that derives the leaf set from the configuration Zod schema and compares it to the inventory artifact, asserting one-to-one coverage, closed-set classification, consumer presence for non-inert entries, and agreement with the existing retired-key, terminal-resource, and field-scope sources. |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/BuilderGate/issues/3 |
| Related Docs | docs/analysis/2026-09-16.wave0-split-contract/00.decision-record.md |

#### Requirement

The repository shall carry a machine-readable inventory that classifies every leaf of the server configuration schema as user-visible, reserved, inert, or safety-only, records the real runtime consumers of each leaf, and is pinned by an automated test that fails when the schema and the inventory diverge.

#### Rationale

Wave 0 has to fix the configuration surface before later waves change it, and no such inventory exists: the nearest artifacts each cover a fragment. RuntimeConfigStore's FIELD_SCOPES and RESERVED_WAVE6_SETTING_KEYS give a user-visible and reserved split but are a runtime table with no inert or safety-only category and say nothing about the leaves they never mention. TerminalResourcePolicyInventory classifies code paths for the terminal resource keys only. retiredSettingsResidue.test.ts covers keys that are already gone. Without a pinned whole-surface inventory, a leaf can lose its last consumer, or gain an unintended one, without anything observing it.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given the server configuration Zod schema, when the inventory is validated, then every leaf reachable in that schema appears exactly once in the inventory, and every inventory entry names a leaf that the schema still declares; a leaf added to or removed from the schema without a matching inventory change fails the validation.
- [ ] AC-2: Given an inventory entry, then it carries exactly one classification drawn from the closed set user-visible, reserved, inert, and safety-only, together with the reason for that classification.
- [ ] AC-3: Given an entry classified as anything other than inert, then it records at least one real runtime consumer identified by module and symbol, excluding the configuration schema itself, the type declarations, the settings facade plumbing, the config template, and test files; and an entry classified as inert records that it has no such consumer.
- [ ] AC-4: Given the inventory is consumed by tooling, then it is stored in a machine-readable form that is parsed rather than scraped, and the pinning test reads that same artifact rather than a second copy of the data.
- [ ] AC-5: Given the inventory is authored, then it reuses the existing classification sources rather than restating them independently: the retired-key list and shipped-surface scan in server/src/utils/retiredSettingsResidue.test.ts, the consumer manifest in server/src/services/TerminalResourcePolicyInventory.ts, and the FIELD_SCOPES and RESERVED_WAVE6_SETTING_KEYS tables in server/src/services/RuntimeConfigStore.ts. A key those sources already classify is not given a conflicting classification here without the conflict being recorded.
- [ ] AC-6: Given a key classified as safety-only, then the inventory records the bound it protects, so that a later change cannot relax it as though it were an ordinary tuning value.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | artifact | server/src/schemas/settingsInventory.json | AC-1..AC-6 | Machine-readable inventory of all 81 configuration schema leaves: user-visible 33, reserved 31, safety-only 16, inert 1. The single inert leaf is pty.maxBufferSize, an input-only legacy alias whose only remaining shipped-surface mention is a comment in binaryFrameCodec.ts. Classification precedence and the representative-not-exhaustive nature of the consumer lists are recorded inside the artifact. |
| VE-2 | test | server/src/schemas/settingsInventory.test.ts | AC-1..AC-6 | Pin suite, 7 tests, 7 pass, 0 fail, 0 todo (cwd=server, npx tsx --test src/schemas/settingsInventory.test.ts). Written red first: the initial run failed with ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND before configSchemaLeaves.ts existed. Sensitivity demonstrated by adding an unclassified leaf to realtimeSchema, which failed the AC-1 coverage assertion; reverting restored a clean tree. The leaf set is derived from the Zod schema at run time by listConfigSchemaLeafPaths, not from a second hand-written list. Review rounds added cases pinning the leaf count, the consumer match kind, and the schema walk throwing on kinds it does not understand rather than collapsing them silently. |
